However, the possibility of schizophrenia (F20) remains due to the presence of delusional ideation, especially if other symptoms such as hallucinations, disorganization, or functional decline are present. The feeling is just so incredibly strong and real to me that no amount of reassurance or logic from others has been able to make me doubt it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices talking about you or felt like you're receiving messages from external sources, such as the TV or radio, that others don't notice?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear any voices or get secret messages from the TV or radio. I actually keep my TV on mute most of the time just to have some light or distraction in my apartment, but I definitely don't get any messages from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs that might seem strange or hard for others to understand, such as thinking your thoughts are being controlled or that someone is remotely influencing your mind?\n\nPatient: I do have a strong belief that others find hard to understand, which is that people in my professional and art circles are secretly collaborating in group chats to ruin my reputation. However, I've never felt like my thoughts are being controlled by anyone else or that my mind is being remotely influenced.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks, have you had periods of unusually elevated mood, increased energy, or feeling like you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that; I definitely haven't had high energy or felt elevated. In fact, it's been the exact opposite—I've felt really low on energy, flat, and just completely unmotivated lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day for the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like going to gallery openings, seeing friends, or even cooking. Most days I just sit in my apartment with the TV on mute, ignoring calls and texts because I can't bring myself to care anymore. When I do force myself to go out, I feel completely flat and disconnected.\n\nDoctor: How long has your motivation been so low and your interest in activities completely gone—has this been happening every day for more than two weeks, and has it made it hard to function at work or in daily life?\n\nPatient: This lack of motivation started gradually over two years ago after my breakup and has gotten worse to the point of being a daily struggle. It has severely impacted my life—I've lost several long-term clients, cut my hours way back at my practice, and barely see my friends or family anymore because I constantly avoid plans.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your suspicious thoughts were less intense, especially in the past two years, did your mood also stay low and empty like it is now, or were there periods when you felt more like your usual self?"

In fact, it's been the exact opposite—I've felt really low on energy, flat, and just completely unmotivated lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day for the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like going to gallery openings, seeing friends, or even cooking. Most days I just sit in my apartment with the TV on mute, ignoring calls and texts because I can't bring myself to care anymore. When I do force myself to go out, I feel completely flat and disconnected.\n\nDoctor: How long has your motivation been so low and your interest in activities completely gone—has this been happening every day for more than two weeks, and has it made it hard to function at work or in daily life?\n\nPatient: This lack of motivation started gradually over two years ago after my breakup and has gotten worse to the point of being a daily struggle. It has severely impacted my life—I've lost several long-term clients, cut my hours way back at my practice, and barely see my friends or family anymore because I constantly avoid plans.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your suspicious thoughts were less intense, especially in the past two years, did your mood also stay low and empty like it is now, or were there periods when you felt more like your usual self?\n\nPatient: It's really difficult to separate the two or remember clearly how my mood fluctuated back then. Ever since the breakup two years ago, it has just felt like a gradual, continuous decline where I haven't really felt like my usual self. I don't think there were any distinct periods of feeling normal, even when the suspicious thoughts were less intense.\n\nDoctor: During the past two years, when your mood was low and you were losing interest in activities, did any of your suspicious thoughts about people plotting against you ever go away completely, even for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, and I don't think those thoughts ever completely went away. There are no features of mood-incongruent or bizarre delusions, hallucinations, formal thought disorder, or manic symptoms. The absence of psychotic features outside the context of a major depressive episode makes schizophrenia (F20) less likely, but not yet ruled out, as mood-congruent delusions can occur in severe depressive episodes. The two-year duration of low mood, anhedonia, fatigue, and functional decline meets criteria for a persistent depressive disorder or major depressive episode. The delusions could be mood-congruent psychotic features of a depressive disorder (F32 with psychotic symptoms). However, the fixed, systematized nature of the delusions—present even when mood was less impaired—and their persistence over time make a primary psychotic disorder such as delusional disorder (F22) a strong consideration, though F22 was not in the original candidate list. Given the current differential and adherence to ICD-10 coding, F20 (schizophrenia) remains under consideration due to the prominence of delusions, but the lack of other first-rank symptoms or disorganization reduces its likelihood. It's just really hard to shake the feeling that they are actively coordinating to ruin me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had these kinds of suspicious thoughts about people plotting against you before, or is this something new that started recently?\n\nPatient: I started having some milder worries about two years ago after a difficult breakup, but it's slowly gotten worse. This intense, constant feeling that they are actively coordinating and plotting against me is newer, and it's been going on pretty much nonstop for the last couple of months.\n\nDoctor: Even though you're aware others say it's not happening, do you still feel certain—like it's definitely true—that these people are plotting against you, or do you sometimes doubt it might be your mind playing tricks?\n\nPatient: I know intellectually that everyone tells me it's not happening, but I still feel absolutely certain that it is true. The feeling is just so incredibly strong and real to me that no amount of reassurance or logic from others has been able to make me doubt it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices talking about you or felt like you're receiving messages from external sources, such as the TV or radio, that others don't notice?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear any voices or get secret messages from the TV or radio. I actually keep my TV on mute most of the time just to have some light or distraction in my apartment, but I definitely don't get any messages from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs that might seem strange or hard for others to understand, such as thinking your thoughts are being controlled or that someone is remotely influencing your mind?\n\nPatient: I do have a strong belief that others find hard to understand, which is that people in my professional and art circles are secretly collaborating in group chats to ruin my reputation. However, I've never felt like my thoughts are being controlled by anyone else or that my mind is being remotely influenced.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks, have you had periods of unusually elevated mood, increased energy, or feeling like you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that; I definitely haven't had high energy or felt elevated. In fact, it's been the exact opposite—I've felt really low on energy, flat, and just completely unmotivated lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day for the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like going to gallery openings, seeing friends, or even cooking. Most days I just sit in my apartment with the TV on mute, ignoring calls and texts because I can't bring myself to care anymore. When I do force myself to go out, I feel completely flat and disconnected.\n\nDoctor: How long has your motivation been so low and your interest in activities completely gone—has this been happening every day for more than two weeks, and has it made it hard to function at work or in daily life?\n\nPatient: This lack of motivation started gradually over two years ago after my breakup and has gotten worse to the point of being a daily struggle. It has severely impacted my life—I've lost several long-term clients, cut my hours way back at my practice, and barely see my friends or family anymore because I constantly avoid plans.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your suspicious thoughts were less intense, especially in the past two years, did your mood also stay low and empty like it is now, or were there periods when you felt more like your usual self?\n\nPatient: It's really difficult to separate the two or remember clearly how my mood fluctuated back then. Ever since the breakup two years ago, it has just felt like a gradual, continuous decline where I haven't really felt like my usual self. I don't think there were any distinct periods of feeling normal, even when the suspicious thoughts were less intense.\n\nDoctor: During the past two years, when your mood was low and you were losing interest in activities, did any of your suspicious thoughts about people plotting against you ever go away completely, even for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, and I don't think those thoughts ever completely went away. However, I can't recall for certain how constant they were early on.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, even during times when your mood was low and you felt no motivation, have your suspicious thoughts about people plotting against you been present almost every day, or have there been periods of several days or more when these thoughts completely went away?\n\nPatient: While those thoughts have been gradually getting worse over the last two years, they weren't always as constant in the beginning. However, for the last couple of months, this intense belief that people are actively coordinating against me has been present pretty much nonstop, every single day. I haven't had any breaks from it recently."

When I do force myself to go out, I feel completely flat and disconnected.\n\nDoctor: How long has your motivation been so low and your interest in activities completely gone—has this been happening every day for more than two weeks, and has it made it hard to function at work or in daily life?\n\nPatient: This lack of motivation started gradually over two years ago after my breakup and has gotten worse to the point of being a daily struggle. It has severely impacted my life—I've lost several long-term clients, cut my hours way back at my practice, and barely see my friends or family anymore because I constantly avoid plans.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your suspicious thoughts were less intense, especially in the past two years, did your mood also stay low and empty like it is now, or were there periods when you felt more like your usual self?\n\nPatient: It's really difficult to separate the two or remember clearly how my mood fluctuated back then. Ever since the breakup two years ago, it has just felt like a gradual, continuous decline where I haven't really felt like my usual self. I don't think there were any distinct periods of feeling normal, even when the suspicious thoughts were less intense.\n\nDoctor: During the past two years, when your mood was low and you were losing interest in activities, did any of your suspicious thoughts about people plotting against you ever go away completely, even for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, and I don't think those thoughts ever completely went away. However, I can't recall for certain how constant they were early on.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, even during times when your mood was low and you felt no motivation, have your suspicious thoughts about people plotting against you been present almost every day, or have there been periods of several days or more when these thoughts completely went away?\n\nPatient: While those thoughts have been gradually getting worse over the last two years, they weren't always as constant in the beginning. However, for the last couple of months, this intense belief that people are actively coordinating against me has been present pretty much nonstop, every single day. I haven't had any breaks from it recently.\n\nDoctor: Have your suspicious thoughts about people plotting against you been present nearly every day for at least part of a year, even if they were less intense at times?\n\nPatient: Yes, I would say the general feeling of people working against me has been there almost daily for well over a year, slowly building up since my breakup. It's only the really intense, nonstop feeling of them actively coordinating that has been concentrated in the last couple of months. But the underlying suspicion has definitely been a constant presence for a long time now."
